is a reduction step of degree
d
,
there exists a term
t^{\prime}
,
a forgetful reduction
R^{\curvearrowright}S:t^{\prime}\mathrel{\rhd}^{*}s^{\prime}

and a step
S^{\curvearrowleft}R:t\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}
.
Graphically:

\textstyle{t\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}}
\textstyle{s\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{t^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}^{*}}
\textstyle{s^{\prime}}

Furthermore,
the step
S^{\curvearrowleft}R
determines the step
S
.
More precisely,
if
S^{\curvearrowleft}R=T^{\curvearrowleft}R

then
S=T
.

Proof.

By induction on
t
:

1.

t=x
:
Impossible, as there are no reduction steps
x\mathrel{\rhd}s
.

2.

t=\lambda x.\,t_{1}
:
The steps must be of the form

R:t=\lambda x.\,t_{1}\mathrel{\rhd}\lambda x.\,s_{1}=s

with
t_{1}\mathrel{\rhd}s_{1}
,
and

S:s=\lambda x.\,s_{1}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}\lambda x.\,s^{\prime}_{1}=s^{\prime}

with
s_{1}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}_{1}
.
By IH we have the diagram on the left, so we can construct the one
on the right:

\textstyle{t_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}}
\textstyle{s_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{t^{\prime}_{1}}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}^{*}}
\textstyle{s^{\prime}_{1}}

\textstyle{\lambda x.\,t_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}}
\textstyle{\lambda x.\,s_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{\lambda x.\,t^{\prime}_{1}}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}^{*}}
\textstyle{\lambda x.\,s^{\prime}_{1}}

By IH, the step
t_{1}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}_{1}

determines the step
s_{1}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}_{1}
,
which implies that
the step
\lambda x.\,t_{1}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}\lambda x.\,t^{\prime}_{1}

determines the step
\lambda x.\,s_{1}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}\lambda x.\,s^{\prime}_{1}
.

3.

If
t=t_{1}\,t_{2}
:
We consider two subcases, depending on whether
the step
R:t\mathrel{\rhd}s

is internal to
t_{1}
or internal to
t_{2}
.

3.1

If
R
is internal to
t_{1}
,
then
R:t=t_{1}\,t_{2}\mathrel{\rhd}s_{1}\,t_{2}=s

where
t_{1}\mathrel{\rhd}s_{1}
.
We consider three further subcases, depending on whether
the step
S:s=s_{1}\,t_{2}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}

is at the root, internal to
s_{1}
, or internal to
t_{2}
:

3.1.1

If the
S
is at the root of
s=s_{1}\,t_{2}
:
Then
s_{1}

is a
\mathbf{m}
-abstraction of degree
d
,

i.e.
of the form
s_{1}=(\lambda x.\,s_{11})\mathtt{L}
,
and
S
is of the form

S:(\lambda x.\,s_{11})\mathtt{L}\,t_{2}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}s_{11}[x:=t_{2}]\bm{\{}t_{2}\bm{\}}\mathtt{L}
.
We consider three subcases, depending on the form of the step

R_{1}:t_{1}\mathrel{\rhd}(\lambda x.\,s_{11})\mathtt{L}
:

3.1.1.1

If
R_{1}
is of the form

t_{1}=(\lambda x.\,t_{11})\mathtt{L}\mathrel{\rhd}(\lambda x.\,s_{11})\mathtt{L}=s_{1}

where
t_{11}\mathrel{\rhd}s_{11}
,
we can choose

t^{\prime}:=t_{11}[x:=t_{2}]\bm{\{}t_{2}\bm{\}}\mathtt{L}
,
according to the diagram:

\textstyle{(\lambda x.\,t_{11})\mathtt{L}\,t_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}}
\textstyle{(\lambda x.\,s_{11})\mathtt{L}\,t_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{t_{11}[x:=t_{2}]\bm{\{}t_{2}\bm{\}}\mathtt{L}}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}}
\textstyle{s_{11}[x:=t_{2}]\bm{\{}t_{2}\bm{\}}\mathtt{L}}

Here we use the fact that

t_{11}\mathrel{\rhd}s_{11}

implies

t_{11}[x:=t_{2}]\mathrel{\rhd}s_{11}[x:=t_{2}]
,
as stated in Lem.
44
.

3.1.1.2

If
R_{1}

is of the form

t_{1}=(\lambda x.\,s_{11})\mathtt{L}_{1}\bm{\{}t_{3}\bm{\}}\mathtt{L}_{2}\mathrel{\rhd}(\lambda x.\,s_{11})\mathtt{L}_{1}\bm{\{}s_{3}\bm{\}}\mathtt{L}_{2}=s_{1}

with
t_{3}\mathrel{\rhd}s_{3}
,
we can choose

t^{\prime}:=t_{11}[x:=t_{2}]\bm{\{}t_{2}\bm{\}}\mathtt{L}_{1}\bm{\{}t_{3}\bm{\}}\mathtt{L}_{2}
,
according to the diagram:

\textstyle{(\lambda x.\,t_{11})\mathtt{L}_{1}\bm{\{}t_{3}\bm{\}}\mathtt{L}_{2}\,t_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}}
\textstyle{(\lambda x.\,t_{11})\mathtt{L}_{1}\bm{\{}s_{3}\bm{\}}\mathtt{L}_{2}\,t_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{t_{11}[x:=t_{2}]\bm{\{}t_{2}\bm{\}}\mathtt{L}_{1}\bm{\{}t_{3}\bm{\}}\mathtt{L}_{2}}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}}
\textstyle{t_{11}[x:=t_{2}]\bm{\{}t_{2}\bm{\}}\mathtt{L}_{1}\bm{\{}s_{3}\bm{\}}\mathtt{L}_{2}}

3.1.1.3

If
R_{1}

is of the form

t_{1}=(\lambda x.\,s_{11})\mathtt{L}_{1}\bm{\{}t_{3}\bm{\}}\mathtt{L}_{2}\mathrel{\rhd}(\lambda x.\,s_{11})\mathtt{L}_{1}\mathtt{L}_{2}=s_{1}
,
we can choose

t^{\prime}:=t_{11}[x:=t_{2}]\bm{\{}t_{2}\bm{\}}\mathtt{L}_{1}\bm{\{}t_{3}\bm{\}}\mathtt{L}_{2}
,
according to the diagram:

\textstyle{(\lambda x.\,t_{11})\mathtt{L}_{1}\bm{\{}t_{3}\bm{\}}\mathtt{L}_{2}\,t_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}}
\textstyle{(\lambda x.\,t_{11})\mathtt{L}_{1}\mathtt{L}_{2}\,t_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{t_{11}[x:=t_{2}]\bm{\{}t_{2}\bm{\}}\mathtt{L}_{1}\bm{\{}t_{3}\bm{\}}\mathtt{L}_{2}}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}}
\textstyle{t_{11}[x:=t_{2}]\bm{\{}t_{2}\bm{\}}\mathtt{L}_{1}\mathtt{L}_{2}}

3.1.2

If
S
is internal to
s_{1}
:
Then
S
must be of the form

S:s=s_{1}\,t_{2}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}_{1}\,t_{2}

with
s_{1}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}_{1}
.
By IH we have the diagram on the left, so we can construct the one
on the right:

\textstyle{t_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}}
\textstyle{s_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{t^{\prime}_{1}}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}^{*}}
\textstyle{s^{\prime}_{1}}

\textstyle{t_{1}\,t_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}}
\textstyle{s_{1}\,t_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{t^{\prime}_{1}\,t_{2}}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}^{*}}
\textstyle{s^{\prime}_{1}\,t_{2}}

3.1.3

If
S
is internal to
t_{2}
:
Then
S
must be of the form

S:s=s_{1}\,t_{2}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}s_{1}\,t^{\prime}_{2}

with
t_{2}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}_{2}
.
Then we can choose
t^{\prime}:=t_{1}\,t^{\prime}_{2}
,
according to the diagram:

\textstyle{t_{1}\,t_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}}
\textstyle{s_{1}\,t_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{t_{1}\,t^{\prime}_{2}}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}}
\textstyle{s_{1}\,t^{\prime}_{2}}

3.2

If
R
is internal to
t_{2}
,
then
R:t=t_{1}\,t_{2}\mathrel{\rhd}t_{1}\,s_{2}=s

where
t_{2}\mathrel{\rhd}s_{2}
.
We consider three further subcases, depending on whether
the step
S
is at the root,
internal to
t_{1}
or internal to
s_{2}
:

3.2.1

If
S
is at the root of
t_{1}\,s_{2}
:
Then
t_{1}
is a
\mathbf{m}
-abstraction of degree
d
,

i.e.
of the form
t_{1}=(\lambda x.\,t_{11})\mathtt{L}
,
and the step is of the form

S:s=(\lambda x.\,t_{11})\mathtt{L}\,s_{2}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t_{11}[x:=s_{2}]\bm{\{}s_{2}\bm{\}}\mathtt{L}=s^{\prime}
.
Then we can choose

t^{\prime}:=t_{11}[x:=t_{2}]\bm{\{}t_{2}\bm{\}}\mathtt{L}
,
according to the diagram:

\textstyle{(\lambda x.\,t_{11})\mathtt{L}\,t_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}}
\textstyle{(\lambda x.\,t_{11})\mathtt{L}\,s_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{t_{11}[x:=t_{2}]\bm{\{}t_{2}\bm{\}}\mathtt{L}}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}^{*}}
\textstyle{t_{11}[x:=s_{2}]\bm{\{}s_{2}\bm{\}}\mathtt{L}}

Here we use the fact that

t_{2}\mathrel{\rhd}s_{2}

implies

t_{11}[x:=t_{2}]\mathrel{\rhd}^{*}t_{11}[x:=s_{2}]
,
as stated in Lem.
44
.

3.2.2

If
S
is internal to
t_{1}
:
Then
S:s=t_{1}\,s_{2}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}_{1}\,s_{2}=s^{\prime}

with
t_{1}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}_{1}
,
and we can choose
t^{\prime}:=t^{\prime}_{1}\,t_{2}
,
according to the diagram:

\textstyle{t_{1}\,t_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}}
\textstyle{t_{1}\,s_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{t^{\prime}_{1}\,t_{2}}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}}
\textstyle{t^{\prime}_{1}\,s_{2}}

3.2.3

If
S
is internal to
s_{2}
:
Then
S:t_{1}\,s_{2}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t_{1}\,s^{\prime}_{2}

with
s_{2}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}_{2}
.
By IH we have the diagram on the left, so we can construct the one
on the right:

\textstyle{t_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}}
\textstyle{s_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{t^{\prime}_{2}}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}^{*}}
\textstyle{s^{\prime}_{2}}

\textstyle{t_{1}\,t_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}}
\textstyle{t_{1}\,s_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{t_{1}\,t^{\prime}_{2}}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}^{*}}
\textstyle{t_{1}\,s^{\prime}_{2}}

Furthermore, to see that the step

S^{\curvearrowleft}R:t\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}

determines the step

S:t\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}
,
it suffices to note that there are no overlappings between the
diagrams,

i.e.
if the step
S
and the step
S^{\curvearrowleft}R

are fixed, then no more than one of the cases above applies.

4.

If
t=t_{1}\bm{\{}t_{2}\bm{\}}
:
We consider three subcases, depending on whether
the step
R:t\mathrel{\rhd}s
is at the root,
internal to
t_{1}
or internal to
t_{2}
:

4.1

If the step
R
is at the root:
Then
R
is of the form

R:t=t_{1}\bm{\{}t_{2}\bm{\}}\mathrel{\rhd}t_{1}=s

and
S
is of the form

S:s\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}
.
Then we can choose
t^{\prime}:=s^{\prime}\bm{\{}t_{2}\bm{\}}
,
according to the diagram:

\textstyle{s\,t_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}}
\textstyle{s\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{s^{\prime}\bm{\{}t_{2}\bm{\}}}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}}
\textstyle{s^{\prime}\,t_{2}}

4.2

If the step
R
is internal to
t_{1}
:
Then
R
is of the form

R:t=t_{1}\bm{\{}t_{2}\bm{\}}\mathrel{\rhd}s_{1}\bm{\{}t_{2}\bm{\}}=s

with
t_{1}\mathrel{\rhd}s_{1}
.
We consider two subcases, depending on whether the step
S

is internal to
s_{1}
or internal to
t_{2}
:

4.2.1

If
R
is internal to
s_{1}
:
Then
R:s=s_{1}\bm{\{}t_{2}\bm{\}}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}_{1}\bm{\{}t_{2}\bm{\}}=s^{\prime}

with
s_{1}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}_{1}
.
By IH we have the diagram on the left, so we can construct the one
on the right:

\textstyle{t_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}}
\textstyle{s_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{t^{\prime}_{1}}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}^{*}}
\textstyle{s^{\prime}_{1}}

\textstyle{t_{1}\bm{\{}t_{2}\bm{\}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}}
\textstyle{t_{1}\bm{\{}s_{2}\bm{\}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{t_{1}\bm{\{}t^{\prime}_{2}\bm{\}}}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}^{*}}
\textstyle{t_{1}\bm{\{}s^{\prime}_{2}\bm{\}}}

4.2.2

If
R
is internal to
t_{2}
:
Then
R:s=s_{1}\bm{\{}t_{2}\bm{\}}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}s_{1}\bm{\{}t^{\prime}_{2}\bm{\}}=s^{\prime}

with
t_{2}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}_{2}

and we can choose
t^{\prime}=t_{1}\bm{\{}t^{\prime}_{2}\bm{\}}
,
according to the diagram:

\textstyle{t_{1}\bm{\{}t_{2}\bm{\}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}}
\textstyle{s_{1}\bm{\{}t_{2}\bm{\}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{t_{1}\bm{\{}t^{\prime}_{2}\bm{\}}}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}}
\textstyle{s_{1}\bm{\{}t^{\prime}_{2}\bm{\}}}

4.3

If the step
R
is internal to
t_{2}
:
Symmetric to the previous case.

Furthermore, to see that the step

S^{\curvearrowleft}R:t\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}

determines the step

S:t\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}
,
it suffices to note that there are no overlappings between the
diagrams,

i.e.
if the step
S
and the step
S^{\curvearrowleft}R

are fixed, then no more than one of the cases above applies.

∎

Proposition 62

(Postponement of forgetful reduction)
.

Let
\rho:t\mathrel{\rhd}^{*}t^{\prime}
be a forgetful reduction sequence and
let
\sigma:t^{\prime}\xrightarrow{d}^{\raisebox{-8.44455pt}{{\scriptsize$\ast$}}}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}
be a reduction sequence of degree
d
.
Then there exist a term
s

and reduction sequences
\rho^{\curvearrowright}\sigma:s\mathrel{\rhd}^{*}s^{\prime}

and
\sigma^{\curvearrowleft}\rho:t\xrightarrow{d}^{\raisebox{-8.44455pt}{{\scriptsize$\ast$}}}_{\mathbf{m}}s
.
Graphically:

\textstyle{t\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}^{*}}
\textstyle{t^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{s}
\textstyle{\mathrel{\rhd}^{*}}
\textstyle{s^{\prime}}

Furthermore,
\sigma^{\curvearrowleft}\rho
determines
\sigma
,
that is,
More precisely,

\sigma_{1}^{\curvearrowleft}\rho=\sigma_{2}^{\curvearrowleft}\rho

then
\sigma_{1}=\sigma_{2}
.

Proof.

First,
if
\rho:t\mathrel{\rhd}^{*}t^{\prime}
is a forgetful reduction sequence and
and
S:t^{\prime}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}
is a single step of degree
d
,
we can construct a forgetful reduction sequence
\rho^{\curvearrowright}S

and a step
S^{\curvearrowleft}\rho
of degree
d

by induction on
\rho

as follows, resorting to Lem.
61

for the constructions of
R^{\curvearrowright}(S^{\curvearrowleft}\rho^{\prime})

and
(S^{\curvearrowleft}\rho^{\prime})^{\curvearrowleft}R
:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\epsilon^{\curvearrowright}S&\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}&\epsilon\\
(R\,\rho^{\prime})^{\curvearrowright}S&\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}&(R^{\curvearrowright}(S^{\curvearrowleft}\rho^{\prime}))(\rho^{\prime\curvearrowright}S)\\
\end{array}\hskip 14.22636pt\begin{array}[]{rcl}S^{\curvearrowleft}\epsilon&\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}&S\\
S^{\curvearrowleft}(R\,\rho^{\prime})&\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}&(S^{\curvearrowleft}\rho^{\prime})^{\curvearrowleft}R\\
\end{array}

The inductive cases correspond to the following diagram:

\scriptstyle{R}
\scriptstyle{(S^{\curvearrowleft}\rho^{\prime})^{\curvearrowleft}R}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{S^{\curvearrowleft}\rho^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{S}
\scriptstyle{R^{\curvearrowright}(S^{\curvearrowleft}\rho^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{\prime\curvearrowright}S}

For the general case, we proceed by induction on
\sigma
,
resorting to the previous construction for the constructions of

\rho^{\curvearrowright}S
and
S^{\curvearrowleft}\rho
:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\rho^{\curvearrowright}\epsilon&\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}&\rho\\
\rho^{\curvearrowright}(S\,\sigma^{\prime})&\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}&(\rho^{\curvearrowright}S)^{\curvearrowright}\sigma^{\prime}\end{array}\hskip 14.22636pt\begin{array}[]{rcl}\epsilon^{\curvearrowleft}\rho&\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}&\epsilon\\
(S\,\sigma^{\prime})^{\curvearrowleft}\rho&\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}&(S^{\curvearrowleft}\rho)(\sigma^{\prime\curvearrowleft}(\rho^{\curvearrowright}S))\end{array}

The inductive cases correspond to the following diagram:

\scriptstyle{S^{\curvearrowleft}\rho}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\scriptstyle{S}
\scriptstyle{\sigma^{\prime\curvearrowleft}(\rho^{\curvearrowright}S)}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{\curvearrowright}S}
\scriptstyle{\sigma^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{(\rho^{\curvearrowright}S)^{\curvearrowright}\sigma^{\prime}}

Furthermore, to see that
\sigma^{\curvearrowleft}\rho
determines
\sigma
,
we proceed in three stages:

1.

First, if

S_{1}^{\curvearrowleft}R=S_{2}^{\curvearrowleft}R

then
S_{1}=S_{2}

by Lem.
61
.

2.

Second, by induction on
\rho
,
it is easy to see that
if
S_{1}^{\curvearrowleft}\rho=S_{2}^{\curvearrowleft}\rho

then
S_{1}=S_{2}
.

3.

Finally,
we can see that
if
\sigma_{1}^{\curvearrowleft}\rho=\sigma_{2}^{\curvearrowleft}\rho

then
\sigma_{1}=\sigma_{2}

by induction on
\sigma_{1}
.
Note that
\sigma_{1}^{\curvearrowleft}\rho=\sigma_{2}^{\curvearrowleft}\rho

then
\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{2}

are either both empty or both non-empty.
The base case is immediate.
For the induction step,
we have that
\sigma_{1}=S_{1}\,\sigma^{\prime}_{1}

and
\sigma_{2}=S_{2}\,\sigma^{\prime}_{2}
;
then note that if

(S_{1}\,\sigma^{\prime}_{1})^{\curvearrowleft}\rho=(S_{2}\,\sigma^{\prime}_{2})^{\curvearrowleft}\rho

then by definition

(S_{1}^{\curvearrowleft}\rho)\,({\sigma^{\prime}_{1}}^{\curvearrowleft}(\rho^{\curvearrowright}S))=(S_{2}^{\curvearrowleft}\rho)\,({\sigma^{\prime}_{2}}^{\curvearrowleft}(\rho^{\curvearrowright}S))

so we have that

S_{1}^{\curvearrowleft}\rho=S_{2}^{\curvearrowleft}\rho

which, resorting to the previous item,
means that
S_{1}=S_{2}
,
and
we also have that

{\sigma^{\prime}_{1}}^{\curvearrowleft}(\rho^{\curvearrowright}S)={\sigma^{\prime}_{2}}^{\curvearrowleft}(\rho^{\curvearrowright}S)

which by IH implies
\sigma^{\prime}_{1}=\sigma^{\prime}_{2}
.

∎

A.3
Proofs of Section
4
— The
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}
-measure

In this section we give detailed proofs of the results
about reduction by degrees stated in Section
5
.

Lemma 63

(Properties of the pointwise multiset order)
.

1.

If
\mathfrak{m}_{1}\bm{:}\succ\bm{:}\mathfrak{n}_{1}

and
\mathfrak{m}_{2}\bm{:}\succ\bm{:}\mathfrak{n}_{2}

then
\mathfrak{m}_{1}+\mathfrak{m}_{2}\bm{:}\succ\bm{:}\mathfrak{n}_{1}+\mathfrak{n}_{2}
.

2.

If
\mathfrak{m}\bm{:}\succ\bm{:}\mathfrak{n}

then for all
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
we have that
\mathfrak{m}\succeq k\otimes\mathfrak{n}
.
In particular, taking
k=1
,
\mathfrak{m}\succeq\mathfrak{n}
.

3.

If
\mathfrak{m}\bm{:}\succ\bm{:}\mathfrak{n}

and
\mathfrak{m}
is non-empty
then
\mathfrak{m}\succ\mathfrak{n}
.

Proof.

The first item is straightforward.
For the second item,
suppose that
\mathfrak{m}\bm{:}\succ\bm{:}\mathfrak{n}

and proceed by induction on the cardinality of
\mathfrak{m}
.
If
\mathfrak{m}
is empty, then
\mathfrak{m}=[\,]=\mathfrak{n}
,
so
\mathfrak{m}=[\,]\succeq[\,]=k\otimes[\,]=k\otimes\mathfrak{n}
.
If
\mathfrak{m}
is non-empty,
then we can write
\mathfrak{m}=[x]+\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}

and
\mathfrak{n}=[y]+\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}

in such a way that
x>y
and
\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}\bm{:}\succ\bm{:}\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}
.
By IH we have that
\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}\succeq k\otimes\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}
,
so

\mathfrak{m}=[x]+\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}\succ k\otimes[y]+\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}\succeq k\otimes[y]+k\otimes\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}=k\otimes([y]+\mathfrak{n}^{\prime})=k\otimes\mathfrak{n}
.
The third item is similar to the second.
∎

Lemma 64

(Higher substitution lemma)
.

Let
t,s
be typable terms and let
x
be a variable.
Then
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t)\preceq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t[x:=s])
.

Proof.

We generalize the lemma for the case
in which
t
may also be a memory.
That is, we prove that
if
\mathtt{X}
is a term or a memory,

s
is a term, and
x
is a variable
then
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\mathtt{X})\preceq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\mathtt{X}[x:=s])
.
We proceed by induction on
\mathtt{X}
:

1.

t=x
:
Then

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},x)=[\,]\preceq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},x[x:=s])
.

2.

t=y\neq x
:
Then

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},y)\preceq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},y)=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},y[x:=s])
.

3.

t=\lambda y.\,t^{\prime}
:
By
\alpha
-conversion, we may assume
that
y\notin\{x\}\cup\mathsf{fv}(s)
.
Then

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\lambda y.\,t^{\prime})=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t^{\prime})\preceq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t^{\prime}[x:=s])=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},(\lambda y.\,t^{\prime})[x:=s])

by IH.

4.

If
t=(\lambda x.\,t_{1})\mathtt{L}\,t_{2}
is a redex of degree
d
:
Then

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},(\lambda x.\,t_{1})\mathtt{L}\,t_{2})=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t_{1})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t_{2})+[(d,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0}))]\preceq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t_{1}[x:=s])+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\mathtt{L}[x:=s])+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t_{2}[x:=s])+[(d,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0}))]=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},((\lambda y.\,t_{1})\mathtt{L}\,t_{2})[x:=s])

by IH.

5.

If
t=t_{1}\,t_{2}
is not a redex of degree
d
:
Then

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t_{1}\,t_{2})=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t_{1})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t_{2})\preceq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t_{1}[x:=s])+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t_{2}[x:=s])=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},(t_{1}\,t_{2})[x:=s])

by IH.

6.

t=t_{1}\bm{\{}t_{2}\bm{\}}
:
Similar to case
5
.

7.

\mathtt{L}=\Box
:
Then

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\Box)\preceq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\Box)=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\Box[x:=s])
.

8.

\mathtt{L}=\mathtt{L}_{1}\bm{\{}t\bm{\}}
:
Similar to case
5
.

∎

Proposition 65

(High/increase)
.

Let
D\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Then the following hold:

1.

If
1\leq d<D

and
t\xrightarrow{D}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}

then
\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t)\preceq\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime})
.

2.

If
0\leq d<D

and
t_{0}\xrightarrow{D}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}_{0}

then
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t)\preceq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},t)
.

3.

If
0\leq d<D

and
t_{0}\xrightarrow{D}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}_{0}

and
t\xrightarrow{D}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}

then
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t)\preceq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},t^{\prime})
.

4.

If
0\leq d<D

and
t\xrightarrow{D}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}

then
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d}(t)\preceq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d}(t^{\prime})
.

Proof.

We prove a more general version of the statement:
in items

2
and

3

we allow
t
to be either a term or a memory.
For example, the statement of item
2

is generalized as follows:
if
1\leq d<D

and
t_{0}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}_{0}

then
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\mathtt{X})\preceq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{X})
,
where
\mathtt{X}
is either a term or a memory.

We prove all items simultaneously by induction on
d
.
Note that:
item 1. resorts to the IH;
item 2. resorts to item 1. (without decreasing
d
);
item 3. resorts to items 1. and 2. (without decreasing
d
);
item 4. resorts to item 3. (without necessarily decreasing
d
).

1.

Let
1\leq d<D

and
t\xrightarrow{D}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}
.
We argue that
\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t)\preceq\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime})
.
Let
X
and
Y
be the sets of reduction sequences

X=\{\rho\ |\ (\exists s)\ \rho:t\xrightarrow{d}^{\raisebox{-7.97224pt}{{\scriptsize$\ast$}}}_{\mathbf{m}}s\}

and

Y=\{\sigma\ |\ (\exists s^{\prime})\ \sigma:t^{\prime}\xrightarrow{d}^{\raisebox{-7.97224pt}{{\scriptsize$\ast$}}}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}\}
.
Note that, by definition,

\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t)=[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \rho\in X]

and

\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime})=[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\sigma^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \sigma\in Y]
.
We construct a function
\varphi:X\to Y
as follows.
Consider a reduction step
R:t\xrightarrow{D}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}
;
note that there may be more than one such step, but we know by hypothesis
that there is at least one.
By commutation (Prop.
18
),
given a reduction sequence
\rho\in X
,

i.e.

\rho:t\xrightarrow{d}^{\raisebox{-7.97224pt}{{\scriptsize$\ast$}}}_{\mathbf{m}}s

there exists a term
s^{\prime}_{\rho}

and reduction sequences
\rho/R:t^{\prime}\xrightarrow{d}^{\raisebox{-7.97224pt}{{\scriptsize$\ast$}}}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}_{\rho}

and
R/\rho:s\xrightarrow{D}^{\raisebox{-7.97224pt}{{\scriptsize$\ast$}}}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}_{\rho}
.
In particular,
\rho/R\in Y
,
and we can define
\varphi(\rho):=\rho/R
.
Moreover,
\varphi
is injective,
because if
\rho_{1},\rho_{2}\in X

are such that
\rho_{1}/R=\rho_{2}/R

then by commutation (Prop.
18
)
we have that
\rho_{1}=\rho_{2}
, given that
d<D
.

First, we claim that

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}})\preceq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\varphi(\rho)^{\mathsf{tgt}})

for every
\rho\in X
.
If
d=1
, this is immediate
since

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}})=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq 0}(\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}})=[\,]=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq 0}(\varphi(\rho)^{\mathsf{tgt}})=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\varphi(\rho)^{\mathsf{tgt}})
.
Assume now that
d>1
.
Then we have that:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}})&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(s)\\
&\preceq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(s^{\prime}_{\rho})&\text{by item~\ref{upper_reduction:ame_increase} of the IH }\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}((\rho/R)^{\mathsf{tgt}})\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\varphi(\rho))\end{array}

To be able to apply item
4
of the IH,
observe that
1\leq d-1<D
holds because
1\leq d<D
.
We resort to the IH as many times as the length of the reduction

s\xrightarrow{D}^{\raisebox{-7.97224pt}{{\scriptsize$\ast$}}}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}_{\rho}
.
To conclude the proof, let
Z=Y\setminus\varphi(X)
,
so that
Y=\varphi(X)\uplus Z
, and note that:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t)&=&[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \rho\in X]\\
&\preceq&[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\varphi(\rho)^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \rho\in X]\hskip 14.22636pt\text{($\star$)}\\
&=&[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\sigma^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \sigma\in\varphi(X)]\hskip 14.22636pt\text{($\star\star$)}\\
&\preceq&[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\sigma^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \sigma\in\varphi(X)]+[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\sigma^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \sigma\in Z]\\
&=&[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\sigma^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \sigma\in\varphi(X)\uplus Z]\\
&=&[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\sigma^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \sigma\in Y]\\
&=&\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime})\end{array}

To justify the step marked with (
\star
),
note that

[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \rho\in X]=\sum_{\rho\in X}[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}})]\preceq\sum_{\rho\in X}[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\varphi(\rho)^{\mathsf{tgt}})]=[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\varphi(\rho)^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \rho\in X]

because
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}})\preceq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\varphi(\rho)^{\mathsf{tgt}})
,
as we have already claimed.
To justify the step marked with (
\star\star
),
note that
\varphi
is injective, so
X
and
\varphi(X)
have the
same cardinality.

2.

Let
0\leq d<D

and
t_{0}\xrightarrow{D}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}_{0}
.
We argue that
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\mathtt{X})\preceq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{X})
,
where
\mathtt{X}
is either a term (
\mathtt{X}=t
)
or a memory (
\mathtt{X}=\mathtt{L}
).
We proceed by induction on
\mathtt{X}
:

2.1

t=x
:
Then

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},x)=[\,]\preceq[\,]=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},x)
.

2.2

t=\lambda x.\,s
:
Then

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\lambda x.\,s)=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)\preceq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s)=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\lambda x.\,s)

by the internal IH.

2.3

If
t=(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}\,u
is a redex of degree
d
:
Then:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t)&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}\,u)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},u)+[(d,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0}))]\\
&\preceq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)+[(d,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0}))]&\text{by the internal IH }\\
&\preceq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)+[(d,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0}))]&\text{by item~\ref{upper_reduction:bme_increase}}\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}\,u)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},t)\end{array}

2.4

If
t=s\,u
is not a redex of degree
d
:
Then:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t)&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s\,u)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},u)\\
&\preceq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)&\text{by the internal IH }\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s\,u)\end{array}

2.5

t=s\bm{\{}u\bm{\}}
:
Similar to case
2.4
.

2.6

\mathtt{L}=\Box
:
Then

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\Box)=[\,]\preceq[\,]=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\Box)
.

2.7

\mathtt{L}=\mathtt{L}_{1}\bm{\{}t\bm{\}}
:
Similar to case
2.4
.

3.

Let
0\leq d<D

and
t_{0}\xrightarrow{D}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}_{0}

and let
\mathtt{X},\mathtt{X}^{\prime}
be
and
\mathtt{X}\xrightarrow{D}_{\mathbf{m}}\mathtt{X}^{\prime}

where
\mathtt{X},\mathtt{X}^{\prime}

are either terms (
\mathtt{X}=t
and
\mathtt{X}=t^{\prime}
)
or memories (
\mathtt{X}=\mathtt{L}
and
\mathtt{X}=\mathtt{L}^{\prime}
).
We argue that
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\mathtt{X})\preceq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{X}^{\prime})
.
We proceed by induction on
\mathtt{X}
:

3.1

t=x
:
Impossible, as there are no steps
x\xrightarrow{D}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}
.

3.2

t=\lambda x.\,s
:
Then the step is of the form

t=\lambda x.\,s\xrightarrow{D}_{\mathbf{m}}\lambda x.\,s^{\prime}=t^{\prime}

with
s\xrightarrow{D}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}
,
so

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\lambda x.\,s)=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)\preceq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s^{\prime})=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\lambda x.\,s^{\prime})

by the internal IH.

3.3

If
t=(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}\,u

is the redex of degree
D
contracted by the step
t\xrightarrow{D}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}
:
Then the step is of the form

t=(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}\,u\xrightarrow{D}_{\mathbf{m}}s[x:=u]\bm{\{}u\bm{\}}\mathtt{L}=t^{\prime}
.
Note that
t
is not a redex of degree
d
because
d<D
,
so:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t)&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}\,u)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},u)\\
&\preceq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)&\text{by item~\ref{upper_reduction:eme_left_increase}}\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{L})\\
&\preceq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s[x:=u])+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{L})&\text{by~Lem.~\ref{lemma:upper_substitution_lemma}}\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s[x:=u]\bm{\{}u\bm{\}}\mathtt{L})\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},t^{\prime})\end{array}

3.4

If
t=(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}\,u

is a redex of degree
d
:
Note that
t
is not a redex of degree
D
because
d<D
.
We consider three subcases, depending on whether the step

t\xrightarrow{D}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}
is internal to
s
,
internal to
\mathtt{L}
, or internal to
u
.
All these subcases are similar;
we only give the proof for the case in which
the step is internal to
s
.
Then:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t)&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}\,u)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},u)+[(d,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0}))]\\
&\preceq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s^{\prime})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},u)+[(d,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0}))]&\text{by the internal IH }\\
&\preceq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s^{\prime})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)+[(d,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0}))]&\text{by item~\ref{upper_reduction:eme_left_increase}}\\
&\preceq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s^{\prime})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)+[(d,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0}))]&\text{by item~\ref{upper_reduction:bme_increase}}\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},(\lambda x.\,s^{\prime})\mathtt{L}\,u)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},t^{\prime})\end{array}

3.5

If
t=s\,u

is not the redex contracted by the step
t\xrightarrow{D}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}

nor a redex of degree
d
:
We consider two subcases, depending on whether the step

t\xrightarrow{D}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}
is internal to
s
or internal to
u
:

3.5.1

If the step is internal to
s
,
then the step is of the form

t=s\,u\xrightarrow{D}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}\,u=t^{\prime}

with
s\xrightarrow{D}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}
.
We know that
s
is not a
\mathbf{m}
-abstraction of degree
d
,
but
d<D
, so it may be the case that
s^{\prime}

is a
\mathbf{m}
-abstraction of degree
d
,

i.e.
reduction at degree
D
may create an abstraction of degree
d<D
.
We consider two further subcases, depending on whether

s^{\prime}
is a
\mathbf{m}
-abstraction of degree
d
or not:

3.5.1.1

If
s^{\prime}=(\lambda x.\,s^{\prime\prime})\mathtt{L}

is a
\mathbf{m}
-abstraction of degree
d
, then:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t)&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s\,u)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},u)\\
&\preceq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},(\lambda x.\,s^{\prime\prime})\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},u)&\text{by the internal IH }\\
&\preceq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},(\lambda x.\,s^{\prime\prime})\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)&\text{by item~\ref{upper_reduction:eme_left_increase}}\\
&\preceq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},(\lambda x.\,s^{\prime\prime})\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)+[(d,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0}))]\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},(\lambda x.\,s^{\prime\prime})\mathtt{L}\,u)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},t^{\prime})\end{array}

3.5.1.2

If
s^{\prime}
is not a
\mathbf{m}
-abstraction of degree
d
, then:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t)&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s\,u)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},u)\\
&\preceq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s^{\prime})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},u)&\text{by the internal IH }\\
&\preceq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s^{\prime})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)&\text{by item~\ref{upper_reduction:eme_left_increase}}\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s^{\prime}\,u)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},t^{\prime})\end{array}

3.5.2

If the step is internal to
u
:
Similar to case
3.5.1.2
.

3.6

t=s\bm{\{}u\bm{\}}
:
Similar to case
3.5
.

3.7

\mathtt{L}=\Box
:
Impossible, as there are no reduction steps
\Box\xrightarrow{D}_{\mathbf{m}}\mathtt{L}^{\prime}
.

3.8

\mathtt{L}=\mathtt{L}_{1}\bm{\{}t\bm{\}}
:
Similar to case
3.5
.

4.

Let
0\leq d<D

and
t\xrightarrow{D}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}
.
We argue that
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d}(t)\preceq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d}(t^{\prime})
.
Indeed:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d}(t)&=&\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{i}(t,t)\\
&\preceq&\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{i}(t^{\prime},t^{\prime})&\text{by item~\ref{upper_reduction:eme_right_increase},
resorting to the IH when $i<d$}\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d}(t^{\prime})\end{array}

Note that for the value
i=d
, we resort directly to
item
3
and not to the IH.

∎

Lemma 66

(Substitution of degree
d
does not create abstractions)
.

If
t
and
s
are not
\mathbf{m}
-abstractions of degree
d
,
then
t[x:=s]
is not a
\mathbf{m}
-abstraction of degree
d
.

Proof.

By induction on
t
:

1.

t=x
:
Then
t[x:=s]=s
is not a
\mathbf{m}
-abstraction
of degree
d
.

2.

t=y\neq x
:
Then
t[x:=s]=y
is not a
\mathbf{m}
-abstraction
of degree
d
.

3.

t=\lambda y.\,t^{\prime}
:
Then
t
is a
\mathbf{m}
-abstraction
but, by hypothesis, we know that it cannot be of degree
d
.
Hence

t[x:=s]=\lambda y.\,t^{\prime}[x:=s]
.
By the substitution lemma (Lem.
34
)

t
and
t[x:=s]
have the same type,
so
\lambda y.\,t^{\prime}[x:=s]

is a
\mathbf{m}
-abstraction but it is not of degree
d
.

4.

t=t_{1}\,t_{2}
:
Then

t[x:=s]=t_{1}[x:=s]\,t_{2}[x:=s]

is an application, hence not a
\mathbf{m}
-abstraction of degree
d
.

5.

t=t_{1}\bm{\{}t_{2}\bm{\}}
:
Since
t
is not a
\mathbf{m}
-abstraction of degree
d
,
we have that
t_{1}
is also not a
\mathbf{m}
-abstraction of degree
d
.
By IH,
t_{1}[x:=s]

is not a
\mathbf{m}
-abstraction of degree
d
.
So

t[x:=s]=t_{1}[x:=s]\bm{\{}t_{2}[x:=s]\bm{\}}

is not a
\mathbf{m}
-abstraction of degree
d
.

∎

Lemma 67

(Lower substitution lemma)
.

Let
t,s
be typable terms and let
x
be a variable
such that
s
is not a
\mathbf{m}
-abstraction of degree
d
.
Then there exists
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t[x:=s])=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t)+k\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)
.

Proof.

We generalize the lemma for the case
in which
t
may also be a memory.
That is, we prove that
if
\mathtt{X}
is a term or a memory,

s
is a term, and
x
is a variable
such that
s
is not a
\mathbf{m}
-abstraction of degree
d
,
then there exists
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\mathtt{X}[x:=s])=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\mathtt{X})+k\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)
.
We proceed by induction on
\mathtt{X}
:

1.

t=x
:
Take
k:=1
.
Then

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t[x:=s])=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},x[x:=s])=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)=[\,]+1\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},x)+1\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t)+1\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)
.

2.

t=y\neq x
:
Take
k:=0
.
Then

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t[x:=s])=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},y[x:=s])=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},y)=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},y)+0\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t)+0\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)
.

3.

t=\lambda y.\,t^{\prime}
:
By
\alpha
-conversion we may assume
that
y\notin\{x\}\cup\mathsf{fv}(s)
.
Resorting to the IH,
we have

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t[x:=s])=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\lambda y.\,t^{\prime}[x:=s])=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t^{\prime}[x:=s])=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t^{\prime})+k\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\lambda y.\,t^{\prime})+k\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t)+k\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)
.

4.

t=(\lambda x.\,t_{1})\mathtt{L}\,t_{2}

where
(\lambda x.\,t_{1})\mathtt{L}
is a
\mathbf{m}
-abstraction of degree
d
.
Note that, by the substitution lemma (Lem.
34
)
we have that

(\lambda x.\,t_{1}[x:=s])(\mathtt{L}[x:=s])

is also an abstraction of degree
d
.
Then by IH there exist
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}

such that:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}&&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t[x:=s])\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},(\lambda x.\,t_{1}[x:=s])(\mathtt{L}[x:=s])(t_{2}[x:=s]))\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\lambda x.\,t_{1}[x:=s])+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\mathtt{L}[x:=s])+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t_{2}[x:=s])+[(d,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0}))]\\
&=&(\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\lambda x.\,t_{1})+k_{1}\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s))+(\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\mathtt{L})+k_{2}\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s))\\
&&+\ (\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t_{2})+k_{3}\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s))+[(d,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0}))]\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\lambda x.\,t_{1})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t_{2})+[(d,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0}))]+(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3})\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},(\lambda x.\,t_{1})\mathtt{L}\,t_{2})+(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3})\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t)+(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3})\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)\end{array}

So taking
k:=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}
we are done.

5.

t=t_{1}\,t_{2}

where
t_{1}
is not a
\mathbf{m}
-abstraction of degree
d
:
Note by Lem.
66

that
t_{1}[x:=t_{2}]

is not a
\mathbf{m}
-abstraction of degree
d
.
Then by IH there exist
k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t[x:=s])&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t_{1}[x:=s]\,t_{2}[x:=s])\\
&&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t_{1}[x:=s])+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t_{2}[x:=s])\\
&=&(\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t_{1})+k_{1}\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s))+(\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t_{2})+k_{2}\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s))\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t_{1})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t_{2})+(k_{1}+k_{2})\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s))\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t_{1}\,t_{2})+(k_{1}+k_{2})\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s))\\
\end{array}

So taking
k:=k_{1}+k_{2}
we are done.

6.

t=t_{1}\bm{\{}t_{2}\bm{\}}
:
Similar to the previous case.

7.

\mathtt{L}=\Box
:
Take
k:=0
.
Then

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\Box[x:=s])=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\Box)=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\Box)+0\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)
.

8.

\mathtt{L}=\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\bm{\{}t\bm{\}}
:
Similar to
case
5
.

∎

Proposition 68

(Low/decrease)
.

Let
D\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Then the following hold:

1.

If
1\leq d\leq j\leq D

and
t\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}

then
\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t)\succ\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime})
.

2.

If
1\leq d\leq j\leq D

and
t_{0}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}_{0}

then
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},t)\bm{:}\succ\bm{:}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},t)
.

3.

If
1\leq d\leq D

and
t_{0}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}_{0}

and
t\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}
,
then for all
\mathfrak{m}\in\mathbb{T}_{d-1}

we have

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t)\succ\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},t^{\prime})+\mathfrak{m}
.

4.

If
1\leq d<j\leq D

and
t_{0}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}_{0}

and
t\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}

then
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},t)\succeq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},t^{\prime})
.

5.

If
1\leq d\leq D

and
t\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}

then
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq D}(t)\succ\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq D}(t^{\prime})
.

Proof.

We prove a more general version of the statement:
in items

2
,

3
, and

4

we allow
t
to be either a term or a memory.
For example, the statement of item
2

is generalized as follows:
if
1\leq d\leq j\leq D

and
t_{0}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}_{0}

then
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},\mathtt{X})\bm{:}\succ\bm{:}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{X})
,
where
\mathtt{X}
is either a term or a memory.

We prove all items simultaneously by induction on
D
.
Note that:
item 1. resorts to the IH;
item 2. resorts to item 1. (without decreasing
D
);
items 3. and 4. resort to items 1. and 2. (without decreasing
D
);
item 5. resorts to items 3. and 4. (without decreasing
D
).

1.

Let
1\leq d\leq j\leq D

and
t\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}
.
We argue that
\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t)\succ\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime})
.
Let
X
and
Y

be the sets of reduction sequences

X:=\{\rho\ |\ (\exists{s})\ \rho:t\xrightarrow{j}^{\raisebox{-7.97224pt}{{\scriptsize$\ast$}}}_{\mathbf{m}}s\}

and

Y:=\{\sigma\ |\ (\exists{s^{\prime}})\ \sigma:t^{\prime}\xrightarrow{j}^{\raisebox{-7.97224pt}{{\scriptsize$\ast$}}}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}\}
.
Note that, by definition,

\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t)=[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq j-1}(\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \rho\in X]

and

\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime})=[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq j-1}(\sigma^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \sigma\in Y]
.
We consider two subcases, depending on whether
d=j
or
d<j
:

1.1

If
d=j
,
let
R
be the step
R:t\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}
.
We construct a function
\varphi:Y\to X

given by
\varphi(\sigma)=R\,\sigma
.
Observe that
\varphi
is injective
and that if
\sigma:t^{\prime}\xrightarrow{d}^{\raisebox{-7.97224pt}{{\scriptsize$\ast$}}}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}

then
R\,\sigma:t\xrightarrow{d}^{\raisebox{-7.97224pt}{{\scriptsize$\ast$}}}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}
,
and in particular
\sigma
and
\varphi(\sigma)

have the same target.
Let
Z=X\setminus\varphi(Y)
, so that
X=\varphi(Y)\uplus Z
.
Note that:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t)&=&[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq j-1}(\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \rho\in X]\\
&=&[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq j-1}(\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \rho\in\varphi(Y)\uplus Z]\\
&=&[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq j-1}(\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \rho\in\varphi(Y)]+[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq j-1}(\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \rho\in Z]\\
&=&[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq j-1}(R\sigma^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \sigma\in Y]+[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq j-1}(\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \rho\in Z]&\text{since $\varphi$ is injective}\\
&=&[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq j-1}(\sigma^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \sigma\in Y]+[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq j-1}(\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \rho\in Z]\\
&=&\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime})+[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq j-1}(\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \rho\in Z]\end{array}

By this chain of equations,
in order to conclude that
\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t)\succ\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime})
,
it suffices to show that
Z
is non-empty.
Indeed, let
\epsilon:t\xrightarrow{d}^{\raisebox{-7.97224pt}{{\scriptsize$\ast$}}}_{\mathbf{m}}t
be the empty reduction sequence.
Then
\epsilon\in X\setminus\varphi(Y)
, so
\epsilon\in Z
.

1.2

If
d<j
,
we construct a function
\varphi:Y\to X

using Prop.
20
.
More precisely, since
d<j
and
t\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}
,
for each reduction sequence
\sigma:t^{\prime}\xrightarrow{j}^{\raisebox{-7.97224pt}{{\scriptsize$\ast$}}}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}

by Prop.
20

there exist a term
s_{\sigma}
and a term
u_{\sigma}
,
such that there is a reduction sequence

\varphi(\sigma):t\xrightarrow{j}^{\raisebox{-7.97224pt}{{\scriptsize$\ast$}}}_{\mathbf{m}}s_{\sigma}

and such that
s^{\prime}\xrightarrow{j}^{\raisebox{-7.97224pt}{{\scriptsize$\ast$}}}_{\mathbf{m}}u_{\sigma}
,
and
s_{\sigma}\xrightarrow{d}^{\raisebox{-7.97224pt}{{\scriptsize$+$}}}_{\mathbf{m}}u_{\sigma}
in at least one step.

First, we claim that

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq j-1}(\varphi(\sigma)^{\mathsf{tgt}})\succ\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq j-1}(\sigma^{\mathsf{tgt}})

for every
\sigma\in Y
.
Indeed:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq j-1}(\varphi(\sigma)^{\mathsf{tgt}})&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq j-1}(s_{\sigma})\\
&\succ&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq j-1}(u_{\sigma})&\text{by item \ref{lower_reduction:ame_decrease} of the IH }\\
&\succeq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq j-1}(s^{\prime})&\text{by high/increase~(Prop.~\ref{prop:upper_reduction}(\ref{upper_reduction:ame_increase}))}\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq j-1}(\sigma^{\mathsf{tgt}})\end{array}

To be able to apply item
5
of the IH,
observe that
we have that
1\leq d\leq j-1<D

holds because we know
d<j\leq D
.
We resort to the IH as many times as the length of the reduction

s_{\sigma}\xrightarrow{d}^{\raisebox{-7.97224pt}{{\scriptsize$+$}}}_{\mathbf{m}}u_{\sigma}
.
The inequality is strict because this reduction contains at least one step.
To be able to apply the high/increase property,
observe that
0\leq j-1<j
.
We resort to this lemma as many times as the length
of the reduction
s^{\prime}\xrightarrow{j}^{\raisebox{-7.97224pt}{{\scriptsize$\ast$}}}_{\mathbf{m}}u_{\sigma}
,
which may be empty.
To conclude the proof,
let
Z=X\setminus\varphi(Y)
, so that
X=\varphi(Y)\uplus Z
,
and note that:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t)&=&[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq j-1}(\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \rho\in X]\\
&=&[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq j-1}(\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \rho\in\varphi(Y)\uplus Z]\\
&=&[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq j-1}(\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \rho\in\varphi(Y)]+[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq j-1}(\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \rho\in Z]\\
&=&[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq j-1}(\varphi(\sigma)^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \sigma\in Y]+[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq j-1}(\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \rho\in Z]\\
&\succeq&[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq j-1}(\varphi(\sigma)^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \sigma\in Y]\\
&\succ&[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq j-1}(\sigma^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \sigma\in Y]&\text{($\star$)}\\
&=&\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime})\end{array}

For the step marked with (
\star
),
note that

[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq j-1}(\varphi(\sigma)^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \sigma\in Y]\bm{:}\succ\bm{:}[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq j-1}(\sigma^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \sigma\in Y]

because

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq j-1}(\varphi(\sigma)^{\mathsf{tgt}})\succ\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq j-1}(\sigma^{\mathsf{tgt}})

holds by the claim above.
Moreover,
Y
is non-empty because the empty reduction sequence

\epsilon:t^{\prime}\xrightarrow{j}^{\raisebox{-7.97224pt}{{\scriptsize$\ast$}}}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}
is in
Y
,
so we may resort to Lem.
63
.

2.

Let
1\leq d\leq j\leq D

and
t_{0}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}_{0}
.
We argue that
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},\mathtt{X})\bm{:}\succ\bm{:}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{X})
,
where
\mathtt{X}

is either a term (
\mathtt{X}=t
)
or a memory (
\mathtt{X}=\mathtt{L}
).
We proceed by induction on
\mathtt{X}
:

2.1

t=x
:
Then

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},x)=[\,]\bm{:}\succ\bm{:}[\,]=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},x)
.

2.2

t=\lambda x.\,s
:
Then

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},\lambda x.\,s)=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},s)=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},s)=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},\lambda x.\,s)

by the internal IH.

2.3

If
t=(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}\,u

is a redex of degree
j
:
Then:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}&&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}\,u)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},u)+[(j,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0}))]\\
&\bm{:}\succ\bm{:}&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)+[(j,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0}))]\\
&&\hskip 14.22636pt\text{by the internal IH }\\
&\bm{:}\succ\bm{:}&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)+[(j,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0}))]\\
&&\hskip 14.22636pt\text{
since $\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0})\succ\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0})$
by item \ref{lower_reduction:bme_decrease}}\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}\,u)\end{array}

Note that we can resort to
item
1
,
because
1\leq d\leq j\leq D

and
t_{0}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}_{0}
.

2.4

If
t=s\,u
is not a redex of degree
j
:
Then

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},s\,u)=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},u)\bm{:}\succ\bm{:}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},s\,u)

by the internal IH.

2.5

t=s\bm{\{}u\bm{\}}
:
Then

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},s\bm{\{}u\bm{\}})=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},u)\bm{:}\succ\bm{:}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},s\bm{\{}u\bm{\}})

by the internal IH.

2.6

\mathtt{L}=\Box
:
Then

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},\Box)=[\,]\bm{:}\succ\bm{:}[\,]=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},\Box)
.

2.7

\mathtt{L}=\mathtt{L}_{1}\bm{\{}t\bm{\}}
:

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},\mathtt{L}_{1}\bm{\{}t\bm{\}})=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},\mathtt{L}_{1})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},t)\bm{:}\succ\bm{:}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{L}_{1})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},t)

by IH.

3.

Let
1\leq d\leq D

and
t_{0}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}_{0}

and
\mathtt{X}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}\mathtt{X}^{\prime}
,
where
\mathtt{X},\mathtt{X}^{\prime}

are either terms (
\mathtt{X}=t
and
\mathtt{X}=t^{\prime}
)
or memories (
\mathtt{X}=\mathtt{L}
and
\mathtt{X}=\mathtt{L}^{\prime}
).
We argue that for all
\mathfrak{m}\in\mathbb{T}_{d-1}

we have

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\mathtt{X})\succ\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{X}^{\prime})+\mathfrak{m}
.
We proceed by induction on
\mathtt{X}
:

3.1

t=x
:
Impossible, as there are no reduction steps
x\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}
.

3.2

t=\lambda x.\,s
:
Then the step is of the form

t=\lambda x.\,s\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}\lambda x.\,s^{\prime}=t^{\prime}

with
s\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}
.
Let
\mathfrak{m}\in\mathbb{T}_{d-1}
.
Then

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t)=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\lambda x.\,s)=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)\succ\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s^{\prime})+\mathfrak{m}=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\lambda x.\,s^{\prime})+\mathfrak{m}=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},t^{\prime})+\mathfrak{m}

by the internal IH.

3.3

If
t=(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}\,u

is the redex of degree
d
contracted by the step
t\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}
:
If the reduction step is at the root,
then
(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}

is a
\mathbf{m}
-abstraction of degree
d
,
and the step is of the form

t=(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}\,u\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}s[x:=u]\bm{\{}u\bm{\}}\mathtt{L}=t^{\prime}
.
Given that
(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}

is a
\mathbf{m}
-abstraction of degree
d
,
the type of its argument
u
is of height strictly less than
d
,
that is,
0pt{\mathsf{type}(u)}<d
.
In particular,
u
is not an abstraction of degree
d

so we may apply
the lower substitution lemma (Lem.
28
)
which ensures that there exists
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}

such that

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s[x:=u])=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s)+k\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)
.
Furthermore, observe that

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},u)\succeq(1+k)\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)
.
Indeed,
by item
2

we have that
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},u)\bm{:}\succ\bm{:}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)
,
so by Lem.
63

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},u)\succeq(1+k)\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)
.
To conclude this case:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}&&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}\,u)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},u)+[(d,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0}))]\\
&\succeq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},u)+[(d,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0}))]\\
&&\hskip 14.22636pt\text{by item~\ref{lower_reduction:eme_left_decrease}
and Lem.~\ref{lemma:properties_of_mgtmap}}\\
&\succeq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{L})+(1+k)\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)+[(d,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0}))]\\
&&\hskip 14.22636pt\text{since
$\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},u)\succeq(1+k)\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)$}\\
&\succ&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{L})+(1+k)\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)+\mathfrak{m}\\
&&\hskip 14.22636pt\text{since $\mathfrak{m}\in\mathbb{T}_{d-1}$,
so $[(d,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0}))]\succ\mathfrak{m}$}\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s)+k\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathfrak{m}\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s[x:=u])+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathfrak{m}\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s[x:=u]\bm{\{}u\bm{\}}\mathtt{L})+\mathfrak{m}\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},t^{\prime})+\mathfrak{m}\end{array}

3.4

If
t=(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}\,u

is a redex of degree
d
,
but not the redex contracted by the step
t\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}
:
There are three subcases, depending on whether the
step
t\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}
is internal to
s
, internal to
\mathtt{L}
,
or internal to
u
.
All these subcases are similar;
we only give the proof for the case in which
the step is internal to
s
.
Then the step is of the form

t=(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}\,u\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}(\lambda x.\,s^{\prime})\mathtt{L}\,u=t^{\prime}

with
s\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}
,
and we have:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}&&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}\,u)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},u)+[(d,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0}))]\\
&\succ&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s^{\prime})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},u)+[(d,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0}))]+\mathfrak{m}&\text{by the internal IH }\\
&\succeq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s^{\prime})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)+[(d,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0}))]+\mathfrak{m}&\text{by item~\ref{lower_reduction:eme_left_decrease}
and Lem.~\ref{lemma:properties_of_mgtmap}}\\
&\succeq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s^{\prime})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)+[(d,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0}))]+\mathfrak{m}&\text{by item~\ref{lower_reduction:bme_decrease}}\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},(\lambda x.\,s^{\prime})\mathtt{L}\,u)+\mathfrak{m}\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},t^{\prime})+\mathfrak{m}\end{array}

3.5

If
t=s\,u

is not a redex of degree
d
:
There are two subcases, depending on whether the
step
t\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}
is internal to
s
or internal to
u
:

3.5.1

If the step is internal to
s
,
then the step is of the form

t=s\,u\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}\,u=t^{\prime}

with
s\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}
.
Note that
s
is not a
\mathbf{m}
-abstraction of degree
d

(because
s\,u
is not a redex of degree
d
).
Hence by Lem.
59

s^{\prime}
is not a
\mathbf{m}
-abstraction of degree
d
.
Then:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t)&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s\,u)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},u)\\
&\succ&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s^{\prime})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},u)+\mathfrak{m}&\text{by the internal IH }\\
&\succeq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s^{\prime})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)+\mathfrak{m}&\text{by item~\ref{lower_reduction:eme_left_decrease}
and Lem.~\ref{lemma:properties_of_mgtmap}}\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s^{\prime}\,u)+\mathfrak{m}\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},t^{\prime})+\mathfrak{m}\end{array}

3.5.2

If the step is internal to
u
:
Similar to the previous case.

3.6

t=s\bm{\{}u\bm{\}}
:
Similar to case
3.5
.

3.7

\mathtt{L}=\Box
:
Impossible, as there are no reduction steps
\Box\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}\mathtt{L}^{\prime}
.

3.8

\mathtt{L}=\mathtt{L}_{1}\bm{\{}t\bm{\}}
:
Similar to case
3.5

4.

Let
1\leq d<j\leq D

and
t_{0}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t_{0}^{\prime}

and
\mathtt{X}\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}\mathtt{X}^{\prime}
,
where
\mathtt{X},\mathtt{X}^{\prime}

are either terms (
\mathtt{X}=t
and
\mathtt{X}=t^{\prime}
)
or memories (
\mathtt{X}=\mathtt{L}
and
\mathtt{X}=\mathtt{L}^{\prime}
).
We argue that
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},\mathtt{X})\succeq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{X}^{\prime})
.
We proceed by induction on
\mathtt{X}
:

4.1

t=x
:
Impossible, as there are no reduction steps
x\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}
.

4.2

t=\lambda x.\,s
:
Then the step is of the form

t=\lambda x.\,s\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}\lambda x.\,s^{\prime}=t^{\prime}

with
s\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}
.
Then

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},t)=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},\lambda x.\,s)=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},s)\succeq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},s^{\prime})=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},\lambda x.\,s^{\prime})=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},t^{\prime})

by the internal IH.

4.3

If
t=(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}\,u

is the redex of degree
d
contracted by the step
t\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}
:
Then the step is of the form

t=(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}\,u\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}s[x:=u]\bm{\{}u\bm{\}}\mathtt{L}=t^{\prime}
.
Recall that by hypothesis
d<j
,
and note that the abstraction
(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}

is of degree
d
,
so the type of the argument
u
must be of height less than
d
,
that is,
0pt{\mathsf{type}(u)}<d<j
.
In particular, the argument
u
cannot be a
\mathbf{m}
-abstraction
of degree
j
,
so we may apply the lower substitution lemma (Lem.
28
),
which ensures that there exists
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}

such that

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},s[x:=u])=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},s)+k\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)
.
Furthermore, observe that

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},u)\succeq(1+k)\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)
.
Indeed by item
2

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},u)\bm{:}\succ\bm{:}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)

so by Lem.
63

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},u)\succeq(1+k)\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)
.
Moreover, note that
t
is not a redex of degree
j
, so:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},t)&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}\,u)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},u)\\
&\succeq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},u)\hskip 14.22636pt\text{by item~\ref{lower_reduction:eme_left_decrease}
and~Lem.~\ref{lemma:properties_of_mgtmap}}\\
&\succeq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{L})+(1+k)\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)\\
&&\hskip 14.22636pt\text{since
$\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},u)\succeq(1+k)\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)$}\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},s)+k\otimes\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{L})\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},s[x:=u])+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{L})\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},s[x:=u]\bm{\{}u\bm{\}}\mathtt{L})\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},t^{\prime})\end{array}

4.4

If
t=(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}\,u

is a redex of degree
j
:
Note that the step
t\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}
cannot be at the root,
because
d<j
, so the redex at the root is not of degree
d
.
There are three subcases, depending on whether the step is
internal to
s
, internal to
\mathtt{L}
, or internal to
u
.
All these subcases are similar; we only give the proof
for the case in which the step is internal to
s
.
Then the step is of the form

t=(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}\,u\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}(\lambda x.\,s^{\prime})\mathtt{L}\,u=t^{\prime}

with
s\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}
, and we have:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}&&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},t)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}\,u)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},u)+[(j,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0}))]\\
&\succeq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},s^{\prime})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},u)+[(j,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0}))]&\text{by the internal IH }\\
&\succeq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},s^{\prime})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)+[(j,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0}))]&\text{by item~\ref{lower_reduction:eme_left_decrease}
and~Lem.~\ref{lemma:properties_of_mgtmap}}\\
&\succeq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},s^{\prime})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)+[(j,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0}))]&\text{by item~\ref{lower_reduction:bme_decrease}}\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},(\lambda x.\,s^{\prime})\mathtt{L}\,u)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},t^{\prime})\end{array}

4.5

If
t=s\,u

is not the redex contracted by the step
t\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}

nor a redex of degree
j
:
There are two subcases, depending on whether the step

t\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}
is internal to
s
or internal to
u
:

4.5.1

If the step is internal to
s
,
then the step is of the form

t=s\,u\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}\,u=t^{\prime}

with
s\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}
.
Note that
s
is not a
\mathbf{m}
-abstraction of degree
j
,
(because
s\,u
is not a redex of degree
j
).
Moreover
d<j

so by Lem.
59

we have that
s^{\prime}
is not a
\mathbf{m}
-abstraction of degree
j
.
Then:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},t)&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},s\,u)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},u)\\
&\succeq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},s^{\prime})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},u)&\text{by the internal IH }\\
&\succeq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},s^{\prime})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)&\text{by item~\ref{lower_reduction:eme_left_decrease}
and~Lem.~\ref{lemma:properties_of_mgtmap}}\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},s^{\prime}\,u)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},t^{\prime})\end{array}

4.5.2

If the step is internal to
u
:
Similar to the previous case.

4.6

t=s\bm{\{}u\bm{\}}
:
Similar to case
4.5
.

4.7

\mathtt{L}=\Box
:
Impossible, as there are no reduction steps
\Box\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}\mathtt{L}^{\prime}
.

4.8

\mathtt{L}=\mathtt{L}_{1}\bm{\{}t\bm{\}}
:
Similar to case
4.5
.

5.

Let
1\leq d\leq D

and
t\xrightarrow{d}_{\mathbf{m}}t^{\prime}
.
We argue that
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq D}(t)\succ\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq D}(t^{\prime})
.
Indeed:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq D}(t)&=&\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{i}(t,t)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(t)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t,t)+(\sum_{j=d+1}^{D}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t,t))\\
&\succeq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t,t)+(\sum_{j=d+1}^{D}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t,t))\\
&&\hskip 14.22636pt\text{removing the first term}\\
&\succ&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(t^{\prime})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime},t^{\prime})+(\sum_{j=d+1}^{D}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t,t))\\
&&\hskip 14.22636pt\text{by item~\ref{lower_reduction:eme_right_equal_decrease},
taking $\mathfrak{m}:=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(t^{\prime})$}\\
&\succeq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(t^{\prime})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime},t^{\prime})+(\sum_{j=d+1}^{D}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime},t^{\prime}))\\
&&\hskip 14.22636pt\text{by item~\ref{lower_reduction:eme_right_nonequal_decrease}}\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq D}(t^{\prime})\end{array}

∎

Proposition 69

(Forget/decrease)
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Then the following hold:

1.

If
t\mathrel{\rhd}t^{\prime}

then
\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t)\succeq\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime})
.

2.

If
t_{0}\mathrel{\rhd}t^{\prime}_{0}

then
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t)\succeq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},t)
.

3.

If
t_{0}\mathrel{\rhd}t^{\prime}_{0}

and
t\mathrel{\rhd}t^{\prime}

then
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t)\succeq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},t^{\prime})
.

4.

If
t\mathrel{\rhd}t^{\prime}

then
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d}(t)\succeq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d}(t^{\prime})
.

Proof.

We prove a more general version of the statement:
in items

2
and

3

we allow
t
to be either a term or a memory.
For example, the statement of item
2

is generalized as follows:
if
t_{0}\mathrel{\rhd}t^{\prime}_{0}

then
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t_{0},\mathtt{X})\succeq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{X})
,
where
\mathtt{X}
is either a term or a memory.

We prove all items simultaneously by induction on
d
.
Note that:
item 1. resorts to the IH;
item 2. resorts to item 1. (without decreasing
d
);
item 3. resorts to items 1. and 2. (without decreasing
d
);
item 4. resorts to item 3. (without necessarily decreasing
d
).

1.

Let
t\mathrel{\rhd}t^{\prime}
.
We argue that
\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t)\succeq\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime})
.
Let
X
and
Y

be the sets of reduction sequences

X:=\{\rho\ |\ (\exists{s})\ \rho:t\xrightarrow{d}^{\raisebox{-7.97224pt}{{\scriptsize$\ast$}}}_{\mathbf{m}}s\}

and

Y:=\{\sigma\ |\ (\exists{s^{\prime}})\ \sigma:t^{\prime}\xrightarrow{d}^{\raisebox{-7.97224pt}{{\scriptsize$\ast$}}}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}\}
.
Note that, by definition,

\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t)=[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \rho\in X]

and

\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{j}(t^{\prime})=[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\sigma^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \sigma\in Y]
.
We construct a function
\varphi:Y\to X
as follows.
Consider a forgetful step
R:t\mathrel{\rhd}t^{\prime}
;
there may be more than one such step, but there is at least one
by hypothesis.
By postponement of forgetful reduction Prop.
21
,
for each reduction sequence
\sigma:t^{\prime}\xrightarrow{d}^{\raisebox{-7.97224pt}{{\scriptsize$\ast$}}}_{\mathbf{m}}s^{\prime}

there exists a term
s_{\sigma}

such that
s_{\sigma}\mathrel{\rhd}^{*}s^{\prime}

and
a reduction sequence

\sigma^{\curvearrowleft}R:t\xrightarrow{d}^{\raisebox{-7.97224pt}{{\scriptsize$\ast$}}}_{\mathbf{m}}s_{\sigma}
.
In particular,
(\sigma^{\curvearrowleft}R)\in X
,
and we can define
\varphi(\sigma):=\sigma^{\curvearrowleft}R
.
Moreover,
\varphi
is injective
because if
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in Y

are such that
\sigma_{1}^{\curvearrowleft}R=\sigma_{2}^{\curvearrowleft}R

then by Prop.
21

we have that
\sigma_{1}=\sigma_{2}
.

First, we claim that

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\varphi(\sigma)^{\mathsf{tgt}})\succeq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\sigma^{\mathsf{tgt}})

for every
\sigma\in Y
. Indeed:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\varphi(\sigma)^{\mathsf{tgt}})&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(s_{\sigma})\\
&\succeq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(s^{\prime})&\text{by item~\ref{shrinking_ame:ame_decrease} of the IH }\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\sigma^{\mathsf{tgt}})\end{array}

To be able to apply item
4
of the IH,
observe that we have
d-1<d
.
We apply the IH as many times as the number of forgetful steps
in
s_{\sigma}\mathrel{\rhd}^{*}s^{\prime}
.

To conclude the proof, let
Z=X\setminus\varphi(Y)
,
so that
X=\varphi(Y)\uplus Z
,
and note that:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t)&=&[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \rho\in X]\\
&=&[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \rho\in\varphi(Y)\uplus Z]\\
&=&[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \rho\in\varphi(Y)]+[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \rho\in Z]\\
&\succeq&[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \rho\in\varphi(Y)]\\
&=&[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\varphi(\sigma)^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \sigma\in Y]&\text{($\star$)}\\
&\succeq&[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\sigma^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \sigma\in Y]&\text{($\star\star$)}\\
&=&\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime})\end{array}

To justify the step marked with (
\star
), note that
\varphi
is injective,
so
Y
and
\varphi(Y)
have the same cardinality.
To justify the step marked with (
\star\star
),
note that

[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\varphi(\sigma)^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \sigma\in Y]=\sum_{\sigma\in Y}[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\varphi(\sigma)^{\mathsf{tgt}})]\succeq\sum_{\sigma\in Y}[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\sigma^{\mathsf{tgt}})]=[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\sigma^{\mathsf{tgt}})\ ||\ \sigma\in Y]

because

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\varphi(\sigma)^{\mathsf{tgt}})\succeq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d-1}(\sigma^{\mathsf{tgt}})
,
as we have already justified.

2.

Let
t_{0}\mathrel{\rhd}t^{\prime}_{0}
and
let
\mathtt{X}
be either a term or a memory.
We argue that
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\mathtt{X})\succeq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{X})
.
We proceed by induction on
\mathtt{X}
:

2.1

t=x
:
Then

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},x)=[\,]\succeq[\,]=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},x)
.

2.2

t=\lambda x.\,s
:
Then

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\lambda x.\,s)=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)\succeq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s)=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\lambda x.\,s)

by the internal IH.

2.3

If
t=(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}\,u
is a redex of degree
d
:
Then:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t)&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}\,u)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},u)+[(d,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0}))]\\
&\succeq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)+[(d,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0}))]&\text{by the internal IH }\\
&\succeq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)+[(d,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0}))]&\text{by item~\ref{shrinking_ame:bme_decrease}}\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}\,u)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},t)\end{array}

2.4

If
t=s\,u
is not a redex of degree
d
:
Then:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t)&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s\,u)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},u)\\
&\succeq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)&\text{by the internal IH }\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},t)\end{array}

2.5

t=s\bm{\{}u\bm{\}}
:
Similar to case
2.4
.

2.6

\mathtt{L}=\Box
:
Then

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\Box)=[\,]\succeq[\,]=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\Box)
.

2.7

\mathtt{L}=\mathtt{L}_{1}\bm{\{}t\bm{\}}
:
Similar to case
2.4
.

3.

Let
t_{0}\mathrel{\rhd}t^{\prime}_{0}

and
\mathtt{X}\mathrel{\rhd}\mathtt{X}^{\prime}
,
where
\mathtt{X}
and
\mathtt{X}^{\prime}

are either terms (
\mathtt{X}=t
and
\mathtt{X}=t^{\prime}
)
or memories (
\mathtt{X}=\mathtt{L}
and
\mathtt{X}=\mathtt{L}^{\prime}
).
We argue that
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\mathtt{X})\succeq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{X}^{\prime})
.
We proceed by induction on
\mathtt{X}
:

3.1

t=x
:
Impossible, as there are no forgetful steps
x\mathrel{\rhd}t^{\prime}
.

3.2

t=\lambda x.\,s
:
Then

t=\lambda x.\,s\mathrel{\rhd}\lambda x.\,s^{\prime}=t^{\prime}

with
s\mathrel{\rhd}s^{\prime}

and

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t)=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\lambda x.\,s)=\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)\succeq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s^{\prime})\succeq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\lambda x.\,s^{\prime})\succeq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},t^{\prime})

by the internal IH.

3.3

If
t=(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}\,u
is a redex of degree
d
:
There are three subcases, depending on whether the forgetful step

t\mathrel{\rhd}t^{\prime}
is internal to
s
, internal to
\mathtt{L}
,
or internal to
u
.
All these subcases are similar; we only give the proof
for the case in which the step is internal to
s
.
Then

t=(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}\,u\mathrel{\rhd}(\lambda x.\,s^{\prime})\mathtt{L}\,u=t^{\prime}

with
s\mathrel{\rhd}s^{\prime}

and:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t)&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},(\lambda x.\,s)\mathtt{L}\,u)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},u)+[(d,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0}))]\\
&\succeq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s^{\prime})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},u)+[(d,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0}))]&\text{by the internal IH }\\
&\succeq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s^{\prime})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)+[(d,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0}))]&\text{by item~\ref{shrinking_ame:eme_left_decrease}}\\
&\succeq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s^{\prime})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},\mathtt{L})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)+[(d,\mathcal{R}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0}))]&\text{by item~\ref{shrinking_ame:bme_decrease}}\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},(\lambda x.\,s^{\prime})\mathtt{L}\,u)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},t^{\prime})\end{array}

3.4

If
t=s\,u
is not a redex of degree
d
:
There are two subcases, depending on whether the forgetful step

t\mathrel{\rhd}t^{\prime}
is internal to
s
or internal to
u
.
These subcases are similar; we only give the proof
for the case in which the step is internal to
s
.
Then
t=s\,u\mathrel{\rhd}s^{\prime}\,u=t^{\prime}

with
s\mathrel{\rhd}s^{\prime}
and:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t)&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s\,u)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},u)\\
&\succeq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s^{\prime})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},u)&\text{by the internal IH }\\
&\succeq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s^{\prime})+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},u)&\text{by item~\ref{shrinking_ame:eme_left_decrease}}\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s^{\prime}\,u)\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},t^{\prime})\end{array}

3.5

t=s\bm{\{}u\bm{\}}
:
There are three subcases, depending on whether the forgetful step

t\mathrel{\rhd}t^{\prime}
is at the root, internal to
s
, or internal
to
u
:

3.5.1

If the step is at the root:
Then the step is of the form

t=s\bm{\{}u\bm{\}}\mathrel{\rhd}s=t^{\prime}

and:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},t)&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s\bm{\{}u\bm{\}})\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)+\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},u)\\
&\succeq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t_{0},s)\\
&\succeq&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},s)&\text{by item~\ref{shrinking_ame:eme_left_decrease}}\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime}_{0},t^{\prime})\end{array}

3.5.2

If the step is internal to
s
:
Similar to case
3.4
.

3.5.3

If the step is internal to
u
:
Similar to case
3.4
.

3.6

\mathtt{L}=\Box
:
Impossible, as there are no forgetful steps
\Box\mathrel{\rhd}\mathtt{L}^{\prime}
.

3.7

\mathtt{L}=\mathtt{L}_{1}\bm{\{}t\bm{\}}
:
Similar to case
3.5
.

4.

Let
t\mathrel{\rhd}t^{\prime}
.
We argue that
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d}(t)\succeq\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d}(t^{\prime})
.
Indeed:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d}(t)&=&\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t,t)\\
&\succeq&\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{d}(t^{\prime},t^{\prime})&\text{by item~\ref{shrinking_ame:eme_right_decrease},
resorting to the IH when $i<d$}\\
&=&\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{m}}_{\leq d}(t^{\prime})\end{array}

Note that for the value
i=d
